, Lem 4.16]
), making our three-functor formalism into a
unitary three-functor formalism
.

5.
An application: Fell absorption for measure groupoids

In this last section, we illustrate the usefulness of our three-functor formalism with a concrete application. We prove that categories of representations of measure groupoids satisfy the Fell absorption principle: the regular representation tensorially absorbs every faithful unitary representation (see Corollary
5.4
for a precise statement).

Hereafter, all measure spaces are assumed to be standard, and all Hilbert spaces are assumed to be separable.
As explained in §
1.3
,
the three-functor formalism constructed in §
4
can be interpreted as a three-functor formalism on the category of measure spaces and measurable maps.

Recall that a
groupoid
is a small category in which all morphisms are invertible.
Given a groupoid
G
, we write
G_{0}
for its set of objects,
G_{1}
for its set of morphisms, and
G_{2}:=G_{1}\times_{G_{0}}G_{1}
for its set of composable pairs of morphisms.
Let us write
s,t:G_{1}\to G_{0}
for the maps which send a morphism to its source and target, respectively.
And let us write
p,m,q:G_{2}\to G_{1}
for the projection onto the first factor, the composition of morphisms, and the projection onto the second factor.
For
i\in\{0,1,2\}
, let us also write
\pi_{i}:G_{i}\to*
for the unique map to the point. Altogether, we have maps:

(23)

G_{2}
G_{1}
G_{0}
*
p
m
q
s
t
\pi_{0}
\pi_{1}
\pi_{2}

A
measure groupoid
is a groupoid such that both
G_{0}
and
G_{1}
are standard measure spaces, and such that all the maps listed in (
23
) are measurable (see
[
ADR56
,
Mac63
,
Ram71
,
Hah78a
,
Hah78b
]
.

Remark 5.1
.

The map
\iota:G_{0}\to G_{1}
which sends an object of
G
to its identity morphism is not required to be measurable. In many examples of interest, the image of
\iota
has measure zero, hence does not induce a map
L^{\infty}(G_{1})\to L^{\infty}(G_{0})
.

We recall the definition of an action of a measure groupoid
G
on a measurable bundle of Hilbert spaces over
G_{0}
:

Definition 5.2
.

Let
H
be measurable bundle over
G_{0}
.
A unitary representation of
G
on
H
is a unitary isomorphism

\alpha\,:\,\,s^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}H\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\alpha}}{{\longrightarrow}}t^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}H,

that satisfies
m^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}\alpha=q^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}\alpha\circ p^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}\alpha
.

If we specialise the above definition to the case when
H\cong\pi_{0}^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}V
for some Hilbert space
V
,
then the relevant data becomes that of an isomorphism

\alpha\,:\,\,s^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}\pi_{0}^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}V=\pi_{1}^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}V\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\alpha}}{{\longrightarrow}}\pi_{1}^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}V=t^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}\pi_{0}^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}V

satisfying once again
m^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}\alpha=q^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}\alpha\circ p^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}\alpha
.

The
regular representation
has
t_{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}\mathbb{I}
as its underlying bundle, and action:

\lambda\,:\,\,s^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}t_{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}\mathbb{I}\to q_{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}p^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}\mathbb{I}=q_{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}\mathbb{I}=q_{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}m^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}\mathbb{I}\to t^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}t_{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}\mathbb{I},

where we have applied base change twice, using the following two fibre product squares:

(24)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}

and

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}

(the associated diagrams of commutative von Neumann algebras are fibre product squares in the sense of Definition
3.2
).

Denote by
(\pi_{0}^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}V)_{\mathrm{triv}}
the Hilbert bundle
\pi_{0}^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}V
equipped with the trivial action (where
\alpha
is the identity). Fell absorption is the statement that the regular representation tensorially absorbs any representation of the form
(\pi_{0}^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}V,\alpha:\pi_{1}^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}V\to\pi_{1}^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}V)
, up to a multiplicity given by
\dim(V)
:

Theorem 5.3
.

Let
G
be a measure groupoid, let
V
be a Hilbert space, and let
\alpha
be an action of
G
on
\pi_{0}^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}V
.
Then the representations
(\pi_{0}^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}V)_{\mathrm{triv}}\otimes t_{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}\mathbb{I}
and
\pi_{0}^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}V\otimes t_{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}\mathbb{I}
are isomorphic,
with isomorphism
u
provided by:

u\,:\,\,\pi_{0}^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}V\otimes t_{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}\mathbb{I}\to t_{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}(t^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}\pi_{0}^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}V\otimes\mathbb{I})=t_{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}\pi_{1}^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}V\stackrel{{\scriptstyle t_{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}\alpha}}{{\longrightarrow}}t_{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}\pi_{1}^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}V=t_{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}(t^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}\pi_{0}^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}V\otimes\mathbb{I})\to\pi_{0}^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}V\otimes t_{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}\mathbb{I}.

(The first and last isomorphisms in the definition of
u
are given by projection.)

Proof.

The map
u
is clearly an isomorphism.
We need to show that it is also a morphism of
G
-representations, ie. that it intertwines the action of the groupoid. This is the content of the following diagram:

s^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}(\pi_{0}^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}V\otimes t_{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}\mathbb{I})
s^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}\pi_{0}^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}V\otimes s^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}t_{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}\mathbb{I}
\pi_{1}^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}V\otimes q_{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}p^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}\mathbb{I}
\pi_{1}^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}V\otimes q_{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}\mathbb{I}
\pi_{1}^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}V\otimes q_{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}m^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}\mathbb{I}
t^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}\pi_{0}^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}V\otimes t^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}t_{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}\mathbb{I}
t^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}(\pi_{0}^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}V\otimes t_{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}\mathbb{I})
s^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}t_{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}(t^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}\pi_{0}^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}V\otimes\mathbb{I})
q_{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}p^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}(t^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}\pi_{0}^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}V\otimes\mathbb{I})
q_{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}(p^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}\pi_{1}^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}V\otimes p^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}\mathbb{I})
q_{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}(q^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}\pi_{1}^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}V\otimes p^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}\mathbb{I})
q_{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}(q^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}\pi_{1}^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}V\otimes\mathbb{I})
q_{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}(q^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}\pi_{1}^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}V\otimes m^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}\mathbb{I})
q_{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}(m^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}\pi_{1}^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}V\otimes m^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}\mathbb{I})
q_{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}m^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}(t^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}\pi_{0}^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}V\otimes\mathbb{I})
t^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}t_{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}(t^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}\pi_{0}^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}V\otimes\mathbb{I})
s^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}t_{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}\pi_{1}^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}V

\pi_{1}^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}V\otimes q_{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}p^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}\mathbb{I}

\pi_{1}^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}V\otimes q_{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}m^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}\mathbb{I}
t^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}\pi_{0}^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}V\otimes t^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}t_{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}\mathbb{I}
t^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}(\pi_{0}^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}V\otimes t_{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}\mathbb{I})
\scriptstyle s^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}t_{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}\alpha
\scriptstyle q_{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}p^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}\alpha
\scriptstyle q_{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}m^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}\alpha
\scriptstyle t^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}t_{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}\alpha
\scriptstyle q_{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}q^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}\alpha
\scriptstyle q_{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}q^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}(\alpha\otimes\textnormal{id})
\scriptstyle\alpha\otimes\mathrm{id}
\scriptstyle s^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}(u)
\scriptstyle t^{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}(u)
\scriptstyle\mathrm{id}\otimes\lambda
\scriptstyle\alpha\otimes\lambda

The middle rectangle commutes because
\alpha
is an action.
The four outer rectangle are instances of item 9)
of Definition
2.3
, applied to the fibre product squares (
24
).
∎

Corollary 5.4
.

Let
G
be a measure groupoid.
Let
H
be a representation of
G
on with
L^{\infty}(G_{0})
acts faithfully, and let
\Omega:=\bigoplus^{\infty}t_{\mathbin{\vbox{\hbox{\scalebox{0.45}{$\bigstar$}}}}}\mathbb{I}
be the infinite direct sum of copies of the regular representation. Then
H\otimes\Omega\cong\Omega
.

Proof.

Decompose
G_{0}
as
\bigsqcup_{i=1}^{\infty}G^{i}_{0}
, such that each
H|_{G^{i}_{0}}
has rank
i
. This induces a disjoint union decomposition of the whole groupoid as
G=\bigsqcup_{i=1}^{\infty}G^{i}
.
On each
G^{i}
, the representation
H
restricts to one whose underlying
L^{\infty}(G^{i}_{0})
-module is trivial of rank
i
.
By Theorem
5.3
, we have
H|_{G^{i}}\otimes\Omega|_{G^{i}}\cong\Omega|_{G^{i}}
.
Assembling all the pieces, we get the desired isomorphism
H\otimes\Omega\cong\Omega
.
∎
